# Search, design, conversion and content findings, 9 November 2026

Awaiting Spencer's approval.

# SEO, GEO and local search audit, condensed from the reviewer's report of 9 Nov 2026
(The reviewer could not save its own report; evidence files are in this folder: notes.md, crawl/, lh/, serp/, local/.)

Summary: on-site technical SEO is excellent. All 57 indexable pages return 200 at both address forms, every canonical and JSON-LD block validates, Lighthouse 98 to 100 performance and 100 SEO on six pages, mobile and desktop. What limits enquiries is off the site: Google Business Profile category, a second firm's listing on the same pin, review count, indexing, directories, links.

F1 Google Business Profile, priority 1. Primary category is "Attorney". Website field is https://spenceralexander.com.au/ which redirects. A Pearsons Lawyers listing sits at "Level 3, Suite 10/1 Main St, Box Hill" on exactly the firm's coordinates -37.8191018, 145.1220999 with primary category "Family law attorney", while Pearsons' own Box Hill page gives Level 9, 990 Whitehorse Road. Nearby firms' primary categories: Robinson Gill "Law firm", Mustard Seed "Legal services", J2 Legal "Law firm", Garry A Nicholson "Attorney". Recommend: check secondary categories; choose primary category with the shared pin in mind, "Estate planning attorney" avoids competing with the family law listing on the pin; add family law, divorce, lawyer, law firm, business law as secondary; if Pearsons does not work from Suite 10, suggest an edit saying so, only what Spencer knows to be true; set website to the www address; add every fixed fee as a service with its price; upload the film and portrait; post each Monday article. About an hour, owner only.

F2 Reviews, priority 2 (counts for internal report only, never on the site): Spencer Alexander 5.0 from 8; Robinson Gill 4.7 from 329; Mahons with Yuncken and Yuncken Blackburn 4.5 from 72; Mustard Seed 5.0 from 56; J2 Legal 4.3 from 11; Oboodi 4.7 from 9; Garry A Nicholson 4.5 from 8. Recommend a review request with the direct link to every client at matter close, reply to every review, never incentivise or gate. The bd repository's closing letter routine could carry it.

F3 Indexing, priority 3. In the one index reachable, only 8 of 57 pages were found: home, service-probate, service-contesting-a-will, service-property-settlement at its extensionless address, insight-probate-victoria, insight-divorce-in-victoria, insight-terms-and-conditions-template-or-lawyer, insight-testamentary-trusts. Never found even by exact heading: the three hubs, fees.html, five service pages. Standard search mode confused the firm with Alexander Spencer accountants in Camberwell. DNS has a google-site-verification record; no Bing verification. service-probate came third of nine for "probate lawyer Box Hill" and the engine's summary named the firm first with fixed fees and the free first call. Recommend: Search Console page indexing report; URL inspection and request indexing for hubs, fees and unindexed service pages; Bing Webmaster Tools import and sitemap; indexnow.py once with all 57 addresses; monthly Search Console export to the Drive folder the site health playbook reads. Confidence medium, one index only.

F4 Directories, priority 4. Yellow Pages listed and consistent but one category, no hours, no reviews, http non-www website. TrueLocal same listing, no reviews. Hotfrog none. LawConnect not listed although its Box Hill pages rank for wills and estates, commercial and power of attorney searches. Word of Mouth none, ranks for "family lawyer Box Hill" and "lawyer Box Hill". Localsearch, StartLocal, Facebook, Yelp none found. LinkedIn company and personal pages refused automated access and no search found either; verify both exist logged out or remove from schema. LIV Find Your Lawyer, Bing Places and Apple Business Connect could not be checked. ABR still shows VIC 3106. Name collisions: Alexander Spencer accountants Camberwell; Spencer Lawyers NSW Central Coast. Recommend in order: LinkedIn check; LIV Find Your Lawyer entry; Bing Places import and Apple Business Connect; complete Yellow Pages and TrueLocal with hours, categories, www website; Word of Mouth and Hotfrog; assess LawConnect; update ABR to 3128; add confirmed profiles to sameAs.

F5 Prices in search descriptions, priority 5. Only fees.html has a price in its description. Proposed descriptions for 7 service pages and the 3 hubs, 120 to 160 characters, using fees.html figures (to be recomputed with any new prices). Service page descriptions live in playbooks/service-content/<page>.json, rebuilt by scripts/build-service-page.py; description, og:description and JSON-LD must match.

F6 Search vocabulary, priority 6. "Custody" appears nowhere on family pages; competitors title pages "Child Custody Lawyer Near Box Hill". "Deceased estate" appears only in the testamentary trusts article; a competitor "Deceased Estate Lawyers Box Hill" page ranks. Proposed sentence for the parenting page and hub: "Many people still call these arrangements custody. The law now speaks of parenting orders, which can set out who a child lives with, the time the child spends with other people and who makes decisions about the child." To be read against Family Law Act s 64B first. Probate lead: "We act for executors and families in deceased estates from our Box Hill office, preparing applications for probate and letters of administration and guiding or taking on the administration that follows."

F7 Service page cap, priority 7. Cap is 8 and the site has 8. Lift to 12, keeping one page per service, Box Hill only, no suburb pages, no price or free offer in a page name. New pages ranked: 1 powers of attorney and medical treatment decision makers ("Power of Attorney Lawyers Box Hill | Spencer Alexander Lawyers"); 2 binding financial agreements ("Financial Agreement Lawyers Box Hill | Spencer Alexander Lawyers", with "prenup" in lead and description); 3 debt recovery and statutory demands ("Debt Recovery Lawyers Box Hill, Melbourne | Spencer Alexander Lawyers"); 4 shareholder agreements and company set up. Estate administration better as a stronger probate page section; consent orders already covered.

F8 Fees page lead should state prices first, priority 8.

F9 llms.txt and schema, priority 9: add ABN and the Google Business Profile link to llms.txt's opening summary; add TrueLocal and any confirmed profiles to sameAs; remove any LinkedIn address that does not exist; consider ABN in the footer, Spencer's call.

F10 Clean links versus .html canonicals: no change; check Search Console's "Duplicate, Google chose different canonical than user" count and bring back to Spencer only with numbers.

F11 Links from other sites: none found beyond Yellow Pages and TrueLocal. Point the PR routine at linkable pieces: the Australian Ageing Agenda piece accepted 5 Oct 2026, LIV publications, eastern suburbs local news, Monash and Sydney alumni pages; research Box Hill and Whitehorse business associations.

F12 Build fault, do now on approval: styles/site.css line 668, rule html:not(.js) .film__frame, sets background assets/video/poster-1600.jpg, which no longer exists (poster is poster-1920.jpg since 28 Sep 2026). 404 on every desktop home page visit at first render; cost Best Practices 4 points. Remove the url() and keep the wine background; bump the site.css version on every page. Do not point it at poster-1920.jpg.

F13 Accessibility: "LAWYERS" line under the header logo, brass #8f6e30 on #fbf9f5 at 10px, measures 4.49 to 1 against 4.5 required, every page; contact.html "fees page" link under What happens next has no underline. Both visible, Spencer's call.

F14 og:image from images.unsplash.com on 48 of 57 pages and schema image on all 36 articles; optional to self host for new articles.

Lighthouse 12.6.1, live .html addresses, mobile then desktop: Home 98/100 perf, A11y 96, BP 100/96, SEO 100, LCP 2.17s/0.46s; Family Law 98/100, LCP 1.68/0.48; Probate service 100/100, LCP 1.61/0.39; Probate article 100/100; Fees 98/100, A11y 95; Contact 100/100, A11y 92. CLS 0 to 0.002. Weight 149 to 329 KB.

Competitors measured: Pearsons Family Lawyers Box Hill 2,618 words, 11 Box Hill mentions, one price, testimonials, awards block; Bentleys Law Family Lawyer Box Hill 2,867 words, 24 mentions, no prices, suburb page; Bentleys Commercial Lawyer Box Hill 3,211 words; Pearsons Probate Lawyers Melbourne 5,544 words with court fee table and long FAQ; Whelan Commercial Lawyers Box Hill 1,026 words; Fogarty Oliver Rothschild fixed fee packages 3,644 words. The firm's hub and service pages: 1,800 to 2,050 words, Box Hill 8 to 11 times, fixed prices, 5 or 6 FAQs with schema, cited legislation, named principal with credentials. Gap is off page.

Checked and sound: status codes, 301s from non-www and http, real 404, canonicals, robots, sitemap exactly 57 pages, feed 36 articles, titles 42 to 71 chars, descriptions 141 to 160, unique, one h1, no skipped levels, alt text and dimensions and WebP on all images, schema valid (LegalService correct, Attorney deprecated), 82 legislation citations, 243 Q and A pairs, breadcrumbs, no self review markup, no broken internal links, NAP consistent, en-AU, answer first leads.


# Design, conversion and acquisition audit, condensed from the reviewer's hand-back of 9 Nov 2026
(Screenshots, measurements, axe results and copy/proposed.md are in this folder.)

Summary: the most refined law firm site found in Box Hill and the eastern suburbs, comfortably beside top tier private client sites on typography, restraint and price transparency. Left to gain: fault fixes and measurement. Costliest fault: on phones the floating Quick exit covers the call and enquiry buttons on every family law page. Thank-you page hides the phone number, wine on wine. Largest strategic gap: no measurement of enquiries or calls by source.

F1 P1 high. Quick exit covers call and enquiry buttons on phones, 17 family pages (/family-law, three family service pages, separation checklist, every Family Law article). Fixed 74px above bottom right up to 880px wide. On iPhone 13, /service-divorce and /service-parenting-arrangements first screen: covers right 88x42px of the main call button and hides the last digit; Pixel 7 /service-property-settlement covers right 88x44 of Send an enquiry. A tap opens Google in a new tab and replaces the page with bom.gov.au. Change: keep it, but on screens up to 880px move it into a slim full width strip above the header or into the header bar beside the menu button (Forte Family Lawyers uses the top strip); desktop unchanged. About an hour of CSS plus one markup change on 17 pages. Show Spencer before and after. Evidence mont/quick-exit-overlap.png, mont/first-phones-a.png, notes/measurements.txt.

F2 P1. No analytics, no call tracking. Enquiry form and both subscribe forms all land on /thank-you, so even thank-you visits cannot separate enquiries from subscribers. DNS has google-site-verification. Revisits the 26 Sep 2026 decision expressly: nothing added to the enquiry and no person recorded, aggregate counts only. Lightest first: (1) free: ask every first caller how they found the firm and record it in Clio with the first fee; read calls, website clicks and directions in the Business Profile performance view and queries in Search Console; UTM tags on every link the firm controls. (2) one script tag, no cookies, no consent banner: Plausible Starter listed at US$9 a month for 10,000 monthly pageviews with goals and custom events, or Fathom US$15 a month for 100,000; count enquiry thank-you, a separate subscriber thank-you, and tel taps via a few lines in scripts/site.js. Cloudflare Web Analytics free and cookieless but no custom events. (3) only if paid search spend is material: Google Ads call assets with call reporting (confirm Australian availability), or dynamic number insertion such as Delacon (no published prices; breaks the single number). Trade off: cookieless counter cannot feed conversions back to Google Ads. Proposed privacy paragraph: "This website does not set cookies and does not use advertising trackers. It uses Plausible Analytics, a privacy focused service, to count visits, the pages viewed, the website that referred each visit and taps on our phone number, without cookies and without identifying individual visitors. Our hosting provider, GitHub Pages, may keep standard technical logs, such as IP addresses and pages requested, to operate and secure the service. The site's typefaces are served from this website itself, so loading a page does not send your details to any font service." CLAUDE.md privacy rule would change too. About US$108 a year.

F3 P2 medium. On all articles the answer first lead sits about 1,020px down at 1440x900 under a 680x425 photograph; iPhone 13 first screen shows none of the answer. Change for approval: lead directly under the byline, photograph after it, in _article-template.html and every article. Half a day.

F4 P2. /contact: first field about 940px down at 1440x900; submitting with no phone and no email shows a fading browser bubble on the email field only and the phone field is never marked; phone field accepts "abc"; "What is your matter about? Optional" reads as one run-on question; "After hours contact is available for urgent matters." never says how. Change: persistent role alert message under the phone and email row with aria-invalid on both; phone pattern needing at least eight digits; Optional in a lighter smaller span; shorten the contact hero for approval; if voicemail exists: "Answered by a lawyer, not a call centre. If we cannot answer straight away, leave a message and a lawyer will call you back within one business day."; Spencer to say how after hours contact works.

F5 P2 medium. Hub hero prices are the least prominent text and orphan onto a second line on the wills hub. Copy inside the existing lead (figures to follow fees.html): Wills: "Wills, powers of attorney and a straightforward probate application each have a fixed fee, from $495 for a single will." Commercial: "The lawyers who draft your documents are the lawyers who defend them, and defined work has a published price, from $495 for a letter of demand." Family: "We settle where agreement is possible and go to court when it is not, and defined steps have fixed fees, such as $1,089 for a joint divorce application."

F6 P2 medium. Fees page first price about 1,197px down at 1440x900, third screen on phones. Proposed introduction: "Each price below is the total you pay, GST included, for the work described beside it, with the amount before GST shown underneath. A single figure is a fixed fee. A figure marked from is the fee for the work exactly as described, and the description tells you what would cost more. We confirm your fee in writing before any substantive work begins, and anything a fixed fee does not cover is estimated in writing first." Also move the Jump to links into the hero for approval.

F7 P1 part one. Thank-you page: "If your matter is urgent, please call (03) 9125 8355 now" has the number wine on the dark wine band at 1.68 to 1. Fix: give .cta__lead a the light underlined treatment .cta__meta a has on the 404 page. Part two P2: noindex subscriber thank-you clone, both subscribe forms' _next pointed at it; heading "Thank you, we will email your checklist."; lead "We will email a copy of the checklist to the address you gave us, along with an occasional plain English update on Victorian law, and one reply to any of those emails unsubscribes you. If something in the checklist needs attention now, your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer." Spencer to confirm how quickly the copy is sent. Add checklist and fees links on the enquiry thank-you page.

F8 P1 high. The wine glow .field-dark::after paints over hero content so the brass call button renders darker. Contrast at the button against 4.5 required: Home 4.47, Fees 4.18, About 4.22, Family hub 4.57; with fix 5.05. Fix: .field-dark { isolation: isolate; } and .field-dark > * { z-index: 1; }, the pattern the footer already uses. Ten minutes.

F9 P2. Reviews: home quotes mostly generic; only one names the work. Review request at close of every matter with https://search.google.com/local/writereview?placeid=ChIJ55iT2dyWG2ERsUO1cPTNK2Y (test once); ask every client, offer nothing; reply in general terms only for confidentiality. Draft: "Thank you for trusting us with your matter. If you were happy with how we handled it, a short review on Google helps other people in your position find a lawyer they can rely on. It takes a minute, and you are welcome to use only your initials. There is no obligation at all, and either way it has been a pleasure to act for you." At the next refresh prefer reviews whose openings name the work.

F10 P2 medium. Referral partner page for accountants and advisers, cloned from the privacy page, no payment for referrals. Opening: "Accountants, financial planners and other advisers send us clients when a family, an estate or a business needs a lawyer. We reply to every referral within one business day, we quote defined work at the fixed fees on our fees page, and with your client's consent we can keep you informed as the matter moves, so the person you sent us is looked after and you know where things stand." Record the referrer in Clio.

F11 P2. Paid search ad groups land on the matching service page with UTM tags; statutory demands to /commercial-law#svc-disputes-debt-recovery-and-litigation; no separate ad pages now; revisit after 90 days of F2 data.

F12 P2. Separation checklist prints to five A4 pages with no phone or address; call prompt hidden in print; empty subscribe box prints. Print stylesheet only: hide subscribe form; print footer line "Spencer Alexander Lawyers, Suite 10, 1 Main Street, Box Hill VIC 3128. Call (03) 9125 8355 or visit spenceralexander.com.au. Your first call is free."

F13 P3. Monthly newsletter with the articles and one fixed fee reminder tied to a life event, review and referral ask for opted in past clients, tagged links. Separation checklist How we help line with fixed fees. A commercial statutory demand checklist is a gap; the lead magnet routine was retired 24 Sep 2026, Spencer's call. LinkedIn: post each Monday article from Spencer's own profile and the company page with a tagged link.

F14 P3. Online booking, call back, shorter form: not yet. Spencer refused a booking page on 8 Sep 2026. Family law risk: self booked confirmation email bypasses the safe to email question. Revisit after 90 days of data; pilot Google Calendar appointment schedules for wills and commercial first calls only.

F15 P1 high. axe-core 4.10.2 link-in-text-block (WCAG 1.4.1): wine links in running text without underline at 1.07 or 1.29 to 1: Home step 2 "fees page"; Contact "Get directions", "save our contact card", "fees page"; About "Register of Lawyers", phone, film link, LinkedIn link. Add .step__d a, .info-block__sub a, .contact-next a, .bio__body p a to the existing underline selector. Contact Office block's last sentence needs its full stop.

F16 P3 copy. h1 works, leave it. "Oversees every matter" appears five times on the home page; proof bar line: "Honours in law from Monash and a Master of Laws from the University of Sydney."; statement body: "Spencer has more than ten years of legal experience across family, estate and business law, so whether it is a first will, a separation or a business under pressure, the advice takes the rest of your affairs into account." About h1: "Spencer Alexander and the firm he leads in Box Hill."; lead: "Spencer Alexander Lawyers acts for individuals, families and businesses in Family Law, Wills and Estates and Commercial Law, and Spencer oversees every matter the firm takes on." Open question since 28 Sep: "tell you where you stand" against the free call not being free advice; option: "Tell us what has happened, and we will tell you how we can help and what it is likely to cost." Merge the two commercial employment pills into "Employment advice and disputes".

F17 P3 polish. Non breaking hyphens in headlines ("step-by-step" breaks after "step-by-" at 1440; "12-month"); 348px gap between costs note and Related reading on hubs and service pages against about 230 elsewhere; orphan card on Resources, four cards in three columns; home practice cards reuse hub photographs, observation only; portrait caption about 4.2 to 1, a touch more scrim.

F18 P2. styles/site.css line 668 references assets/video/poster-1600.jpg, removed 28 Sep 2026; 404 on every home page load. (The SEO reviewer recommends removing the url() rather than pointing at poster-1920.jpg, so no visitor downloads the 91 KB poster at first paint; prefer that.)

Comparison: Robinson Gill (hero blocked automation; Chinese language switch; Let's talk); Silk Legal template site with Free consultation; MS and Co Lawyers pop up and Book now; Forte Family Lawyers team photograph, booking, recognition logos, top strip quick exit on phones; Hall & Wilcox, Withers, Mishcon de Reya restrained, two behind cookie banners. The firm has the best typography and hierarchy locally and the clearest prices, no cookie banner.

Checked and sound: live equals checkout; 56 full page screenshots, no overflow or clipped text; phone first screens show headline, promise and both buttons; one h1, alt text everywhere; skip link first, 3px focus outline; tap targets pass WCAG 2.2 AA; reduced motion respected, film click to play; contrast on dark bands, of 626 elements only F7 and F8 fail; form labels, autocomplete, phone or email check, honeypot and the Melbourne time subject stamp work; prices outside fees.html match; 404 sound; home about 207 KB in 11 requests on phone; settled choices intact.


# Content and style review, 52 pages


## insight-agreed-property-settlement.html
Scores: voice 8, clarity 8, persuasion 8, answer first 8

One of the two strongest of the six. It uses question headings and direct 'No.' answers, puts a mid-article call prompt straight after the time limit, and quotes the $3,025 fixed fee exactly, linked to the property settlement service page. What holds it back: one 64-word sentence listing the agreement's requirements, a run of fragment signposts in the four-part section, a staged opening, and three missed chances to send the reader somewhere useful. Those are the enforcement guide, which no article links at all, the binding financial agreement fee anchor, and the fee in the closing prompt.

- [medium] clarity: A 64-word sentence stacks the four requirements for a binding financial agreement. The checker flags it, and a worried reader has to read it twice to pick out what the requirements are.
  Current: It is binding if it is signed by both of you, each of you received independent legal advice from a lawyer before signing about its effect on your rights and its advantages and disadvantages, each of you was given a statement signed by your lawyer that the advice was given, and a copy of each statement went to the other person or their lawyer.
  Proposed: It is binding if both of you sign it after each of you has received independent legal advice from a lawyer about its effect on your rights and its advantages and disadvantages. For it to be binding, each of you must also be given a statement signed by your lawyer that the advice was given, and a copy of each statement must go to the other person or their lawyer.
- [medium] fees-and-links: The follow-through paragraph is where a reader learns that the transfers must happen by the dates in the orders, and it is the natural place to link the enforcement guide. At present insight-enforcing-property-orders.html is linked from no article at all, only from the hub, the home page, the insights page and About.
  Current: Fourth, the follow through: lodging the application, answering any question the court raises, and making sure the transfers and payments happen by the dates the sealed orders set.
  Proposed: The fourth is the follow through: lodging the application, answering any question the court raises, and making sure the transfers and payments happen by the dates the sealed orders set. If a step is still missed after the orders are made, our guide to enforcing property orders explains what the court can do. (Link the last six words to /insight-enforcing-property-orders.)
- [low] voice: The four-part section opens with fragments: 'Less negotiating and more checking, in four parts.', then 'First, the pool.', 'Second, the test.', 'Third, the mechanics.' and 'Fourth, the follow through:'. This reads clipped. The test paragraph also uses the stock word 'framework'.
  Current: Less negotiating and more checking, in four parts. / First, the pool. / Second, the test. / Third, the mechanics. / so a lawyer measures your agreement against the framework
  Proposed: Most of the work is checking rather than negotiating, and it falls into four parts. / The first is the pool. / The second is the legal test. / The third is the mechanics. / so a lawyer measures your agreement against that test
- [low] voice: The sentence builds up to its point and then reveals it after a colon.
  Current: What changes in the meantime is the property itself: the court looks at what exists when it deals with the application, so savings, superannuation and the value of the home keep moving while nothing is final.
  Proposed: In the meantime the property itself keeps changing. The court looks at what exists when it deals with the application, so savings, superannuation and the value of the home keep moving while nothing is final.
- [low] fees-and-links: The binding financial agreement price has no 'including GST', although the consent orders sentence before it has one. Both fee links open the top of the fees page rather than the fee ids the site created for this purpose.
  Current: A binding financial agreement is from $3,025 per party, and our fees page sets out what it covers and what costs more.
  Proposed: A binding financial agreement is from $3,025 per party including GST, and our fees page sets out what it covers and what costs more. (Link 'fees page' to /fees#fee-binding-financial-agreement. The consent orders link can stay on the service page.)
- [low] persuasion: The closing prompt is aimed at the reader who is ready to act, but it leaves out the one fact that settles the decision, the fixed fee.
  Current: Reached an agreement and ready to make it final? We prepare consent orders and financial agreements from the terms you have already settled. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
  Proposed: Reached an agreement and ready to make it final? We prepare consent orders for a fixed fee of $3,025 including GST, and financial agreements from the terms you have already settled. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.

## insight-binding-financial-agreements.html
Scores: voice 5, clarity 7, persuasion 6, answer first 6

The oldest of the six (27 Apr 2026) and the furthest from Spencer's voice. The law is sound and the lead answers well. The body leans on metaphor and rhetoric: ousts, mirror provisions, essential machinery, bless, near-bulletproof, earn their keep, bespoke, load-bearing wall and romantic poison. Five of its ten headings are labels rather than questions, and two sections are fragment bullet lists. It closes with a 'bottom line' that says a well-made agreement 'holds', which overstates what the article itself explains. It never links the agreed settlement article, which covers the choice between a BFA and consent orders in more depth, and its fee wording, 'quoted in writing' and 'certificate', departs from the rest of the site.

- [high] voice: 'The bottom line' is a stock heading the checker flags. The closing summary repeats the article in slogans. 'Made early, honestly and with independent advice, it holds' states as certain what the set-aside section says is not, because an agreement can still be set aside for changed circumstances or a child's hardship however well it was made. That comes close to promising an outcome.
  Current: A binding financial agreement is neither romance insurance nor a magic shield: it is a serious contract that rewards serious preparation. Made early, honestly and with independent advice, it holds. Made under pressure with recycled templates, it fails exactly when needed. Decide which kind you are making before you start.
  Proposed: Delete the 'The bottom line' heading, its paragraph and its entry in the contents list, and let the closing call prompt follow How we help. If Spencer wants a closing line, use: An agreement made early, on full disclosure and with independent advice on both sides is much harder to challenge than one signed under pressure, so start months before the wedding or the move rather than weeks before it.
- [medium] structure: These headings are labels, while the newer articles use the questions an anxious reader types, which also suits answer engines. 'Where we fit' and 'Questions we are asked about BFAs' also differ from the 'How we help' and 'Common questions' headings every newer article uses.
  Current: The non-negotiable formalities / What a good agreement covers / The conversation is the hard part / Questions we are asked about BFAs / Where we fit
  Proposed: What makes a financial agreement binding? / What should a binding financial agreement cover? / How do you raise a prenup with your partner? / Common questions / How we help (with the contents list and each h2 id updated to the new slug)
- [medium] voice: The opening section stages its point behind a colon, uses 'ousts' and the stock word 'framework', repeats 'precisely' for effect, and tacks the Act on as a separate last sentence.
  Current: Its defining feature is what it excludes: a properly made BFA ousts the court's power to divide your property under the usual just-and-equitable framework. You are choosing your own rules in place of the court's. That is precisely its appeal, and precisely why the law surrounds it with formality. The law in question is the Family Law Act 1975 (Cth), under which these agreements are made.
  Proposed: A properly made agreement excludes the court's power to divide your property under the usual just and equitable test, so you are choosing your own rules in place of the court's. The Family Law Act 1975 (Cth), under which these agreements are made, surrounds them with strict formalities for exactly that reason.
- [medium] voice: The timing section is three fragment bullets, one with two colons in a row, followed by two short aphorisms. Three options read more naturally as prose.
  Current: During a relationship: commonly where circumstances change: a business is founded, an inheritance lands, or a couple reconciles on agreed financial terms. ... De facto couples, including same-sex couples, have their own mirror provisions. The timing changes the drafting, not the essential machinery.
  Proposed: You can make one before you marry or move in together, which is the classic prenup, often protecting assets built up before the relationship, an expected inheritance or children of a first marriage. You can make one during the relationship, commonly when circumstances change, such as when a business is founded, an inheritance arrives or a couple reconciles on agreed financial terms. And you can make one after separation or divorce, as one of the two ways to finalise a property settlement without going to court. De facto couples, including same-sex couples, have their own parallel provisions in the Act, and the timing changes how the agreement is drafted rather than how it works. (Keep the property settlement link to /service-property-settlement.)
- [medium] overlap: The first sentence does not answer the heading. The paragraph uses slang and stock words (near-bulletproof, bless, earn their keep, bespoke, fashionable) and includes an aside about the court's website. It also repeats the comparison that insight-agreed-property-settlement.html makes in full, and property-after-separation makes a third time, without linking the fuller treatment.
  Current: Separated couples finalising property have two instruments. Consent orders are approved by the Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia, which checks the deal is just and equitable and explains on its website how to apply; approval brings the court's authority and near-bulletproof finality. ... Broadly: consent orders are the default for post-separation settlements; BFAs earn their keep before and during relationships, and in bespoke situations. We advise on which fits, not which is fashionable.
  Proposed: For a separated couple who agree, consent orders are usually the simpler route. They are made by the Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia, which checks that the division is just and equitable, and once made they carry the court's authority and are very hard to disturb. A BFA involves no court and no fairness check, which makes it faster and more private, and it suits arrangements a court might not approve, or couples who have not separated at all, for whom consent orders are generally not available. BFAs are therefore most useful before and during a relationship, and in unusual situations after one. Our guide to making an agreed settlement final compares the two in detail, and we advise on which suits you. (Link to /insight-agreed-property-settlement.)
- [medium] voice: No worried client would ask 'Is a prenup romantic poison?'. The one-lawyer answer is made of metaphor and never states the rule, which the agreed settlement article states plainly. 'Reviews are cheap; challenges are not' is a slogan. The FAQPage schema must change with any FAQ wording.
  Current: Is a prenup romantic poison? / No, and any arrangement that pretends otherwise produces an agreement built to fail. Independence of advice is the load-bearing wall of the whole structure. / Reviews are cheap; challenges are not.
  Proposed: Will asking for a prenup damage our relationship? / No. The Act requires each of you to receive independent legal advice before signing, and one lawyer cannot advise both of you independently, so an agreement made that way is generally not binding. / ...is a statutory ground for setting agreements aside, so have the agreement reviewed whenever your circumstances change.
- [medium] accuracy: The first sentence is an unsourced claim about the whole profession, and because the site names no cases, the reader cannot check it. 'In this territory' reads like a reference to a Territory. 'The lesson is not X. It is Y.' is the set-up-and-knock-down structure the voice rules reject.
  Current: The High Court's treatment of pressure in this territory reset drafting practice across the profession. The lesson is not that BFAs are futile. It is that they must be prepared unhurriedly, on full disclosure, with independent advice on both sides, and reviewed when life changes.
  Proposed: None of this makes an agreement futile, but it does mean an agreement must be prepared without haste, on full disclosure, with independent advice on both sides, and reviewed when life changes.
- [medium] consistency: 'Quoted in writing before we begin' departs from the site's settled wording, a written fee estimate before substantive work begins, which the rail card on this page and fees.html both use. The 20 Oct 2026 accuracy run flagged the same drift on the business structures article. The section says 'certificate' where the formalities section says 'statement', the Act's word. The fee link opens the family law section rather than the BFA fee, and the section ends on a fragment.
  Current: Spencer Alexander Lawyers drafts financial agreements, provides the independent advice and certificate for agreements drafted elsewhere, and advises on setting aside agreements that should never have been signed. ... Other work is quoted in writing before we begin. If your situation is post-separation, read how property is divided and the 2025 reforms first, then talk to us about which instrument, BFA or consent orders, suits you. More at our family law page.
  Proposed: Spencer Alexander Lawyers drafts financial agreements, gives the independent advice and signed statement the Act requires on agreements drafted elsewhere, and advises on setting aside agreements that should never have been signed. Our fee for a binding financial agreement is from $3,025 per party including GST, and our fees page sets out what that covers and what costs more. Other work is estimated in writing before any substantive work begins. If you have already separated, our guides to making an agreed settlement final, to how property is divided and to the 2025 reforms explain the alternatives, and we can advise on whether a binding financial agreement or consent orders suits you better. (Link 'fees page' to /fees#fee-binding-financial-agreement. Note that fees.html itself says 'certificate of independent legal advice'.)
- [low] voice: The opening image is overwritten, and 'navigate' is a stock word.
  Current: Raising a BFA with someone you love can feel like planning the funeral at the engagement party. In practice, couples who navigate it well treat it as financial transparency rather than distrust:
  Proposed: Raising a prenup with someone you love can feel uncomfortable. Couples who handle it well treat it as financial openness rather than distrust: both partners see everything, both get independent advice and both know where they stand. Started months rather than weeks before a wedding, it becomes one honest conversation instead of a standing grievance.

## insight-child-support-australia.html
Scores: voice 7, clarity 8, persuasion 6, answer first 8

Clear, well ordered and genuinely useful. The headings are questions, the numbered formula is a list the content genuinely calls for, and the lead names both deadlines. The weak points are a reforms section dated 'As at August 2026', which has passed and disagrees with the September currency line, and the gate misses it because of the capital A. The mid-article prompt sits before the 28-day objection deadline rather than after it. There are a few machine patterns and one unsourced claim about why parents lose. The How we help section says nothing about fees and does not link the service pages.

- [high] stale: The 'As at August 2026' date has passed and disagrees with the currency line, which says September 2026. The gate check 'every dated currency phrase matches the currency line' passed in today's run because its pattern is case-sensitive and this phrase starts with a capital A. By November 2026 some measures may have been legislated or commenced, so the accuracy routine should re-verify their status before any rewrite; I have not been able to (legal query). 'Described as' does not say by whom, and the last sentence sends the reader to the currency line instead of saying what applies.
  Current: As at August 2026, the Australian Government has announced a package of reforms to the Child Support Scheme, described as the most significant changes since 2008 and to be implemented progressively from 2026. ... Everything above reflects the system as it operates at the date in the currency line at the end of this guide.
  Proposed: Once the status is re-verified: The Australian Government has announced a package of reforms to the Child Support Scheme, to be implemented progressively from 2026 and described as the most significant changes since 2008. ... Until each change commences, the rules described above continue to apply. (Name whoever gave the 'most significant' description once it is confirmed. Update the description's 2026 reforms wording and the dates if anything has commenced.)
- [medium] call-prompt: The site's rule puts the mid-article prompt straight after the section that states a time limit. Here it sits after the care section, before the change of assessment and objection sections, so the 28-day objection deadline has no prompt after it. The prompt also opens with a fragment that drops its article and uses a contraction the rest of the page avoids.
  Current: Assessment doesn't match your real circumstances? We can tell you which route fits. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
  Proposed: Move the callout to directly after 'How do I object to a child support decision?' and reword it: Received a child support decision you think is wrong? You generally have 28 days from receiving it to object. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
- [medium] accuracy: This is a statistical claim with no source. The point persuades better through the concrete deadline itself.
  Current: The most common reason a parent loses a good argument is letting these short windows run out.
  Proposed: A parent with a good argument can lose it simply by letting one of these short windows pass.
- [medium] voice: 'One point has to be said plainly:' announces plainness instead of being plain. 'That is why' comments on the sentence before it, and 'meaningfully' is a stock word that tells the reader nothing.
  Current: and crossing into shared care changes the calculation meaningfully. That is why arguments about nights become arguments about money, and why arrangements are worth recording properly. Our guide to parenting arrangements after separation covers how. / One point has to be said plainly: care and money are separate legal obligations.
  Proposed: and moving into shared care changes the result considerably. Arguments about nights therefore often become arguments about money, so the care arrangements are worth recording properly, as our guide to parenting arrangements after separation explains. / Care and money are separate legal obligations.
- [medium] fees-and-links: The only section that sells the firm says nothing about cost, although child support work is not on the fixed fee list. 'Property settlement' links an article rather than the service page, parenting arrangements is not linked, and the family law link misses the hub's child support block. 'Rarely travels alone' is a metaphor.
  Current: Child support rarely travels alone, so we deal with it alongside property settlement and parenting arrangements. ... Learn more about our family law practice.
  Proposed: Child support is rarely the only issue after separation, so we deal with it alongside property settlement and parenting arrangements. Child support work is not on our fixed fee list, so you receive a written fee estimate before any substantive work begins. (Link property settlement to /service-property-settlement, parenting arrangements to /service-parenting-arrangements, and family law practice to /family-law#svc-child-support-and-spousal-maintenance.)
- [low] clarity: 'They' follows the limited agreement item, which needs no legal advice, so the reader cannot tell which agreements fail for want of independent advice.
  Current: They fail for the same reasons binding financial agreements do: rushed drafting, advice that was not independent, or terms that do not survive a change nobody planned for.
  Proposed: Binding child support agreements fail for the same reasons binding financial agreements do: rushed drafting, advice that was not independent, or terms that do not survive a change nobody planned for.
- [low] call-prompt: The prompt promises what one conversation will establish, in place of the site's settled free first call wording.
  Current: Facing a child support assessment that does not match your circumstances? One conversation tells you whether you have a case. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
  Proposed: Facing a child support assessment that does not match your circumstances? Your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer who will tell you plainly whether a change of assessment or an objection is worth pursuing. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
- [low] legal-query: The page may be missing a time point. My understanding is that when collection switches from private collection, Services Australia will generally collect only recent arrears, about three months, or up to nine in special circumstances. If so, switching promptly is a deadline the page should name. I have not verified this because servicesaustralia.gov.au is blocked. The accuracy routine should read the Child Support (Registration and Collection) Act 1988 before anything is added. Not rewritten.
  Current: If you are collecting privately and payments have stopped, switching to Services Australia collection is usually the first practical move: those powers only become available once the agency is collecting.
  Proposed: No rewrite until verified.

## insight-de-facto-separation.html
Scores: voice 6, clarity 6, persuasion 7, answer first 7

It covers the right ground and has a well-placed mid-article prompt and the correct consent orders fee, but it reads older than the newer articles. Seven of its eleven headings are labels. The definition of a de facto relationship is imprecise in a way that matters to anyone still married to someone else. The maintenance sentence can be read as giving married couples a two-year limit. One sentence runs to 77 words, the grandparents link is a non sequitur, the child support guide goes unlinked, and the bottom line ends on a saying. The lead sits at exactly 70 words, so any change to it must not lengthen it.

- [high] legal-query: I read section 4AA of the Family Law Act 1975 (Cth), compilation 101, today. Section 4AA(1)(a) requires that 'the persons are not legally married to each other', and section 4AA(5)(b) says a de facto relationship 'can exist even if one of the persons is legally married to someone else or in another de facto relationship'. 'You are not married' can be read as not married to anyone. That would wrongly tell a reader who has separated but not divorced, and now lives with a new partner, that they cannot be in a de facto relationship. Not rewritten; this is for Spencer and the second check that legal corrections need.
  Current: Under the Family Law Act, you are in a de facto relationship if you are not married or related by family, and you have a relationship as a couple living together on a genuine domestic basis.
  Proposed: No substantive rewrite proposed. The Act's own words are 'not legally married to each other'.
- [medium] clarity: Read next to 'spousal maintenance in marriage', 'the same two-year time limit' suggests married couples also have two years. Section 44(3) gives them 12 months from the divorce taking effect, and the two years in section 44(5) is the de facto limit for both property and maintenance. The closing link sentence is clipped.
  Current: It is the same principle as spousal maintenance in marriage, subject to the same two-year time limit, and often resolved inside the broader property settlement rather than as a standalone fight. Read our guide to spousal maintenance in Victoria.
  Proposed: The principle is the same as spousal maintenance in a marriage, but a de facto claim is subject to the same two-year time limit as a de facto property claim, and it is often resolved within the broader property settlement rather than argued on its own. Our guide to spousal maintenance in Victoria explains the test and the time limits for married and de facto partners.
- [medium] clarity: This 77-word sentence nests 'either' and 'or' clauses. The checker flags it, and it needs rereading.
  Current: A surviving de facto partner is treated in the same way as a spouse under Victoria's intestacy and family provision rules only if the relationship was a registered relationship at the date of death, or the couple were living together as a couple on a genuine domestic basis when the deceased died and either had done so continuously for at least two years or have a child together who was under 18 at the date of death.
  Proposed: Under Victoria's intestacy and family provision rules, a surviving de facto partner is treated in the same way as a spouse only if the relationship was registered at the date of death or, if it was not, the couple were living together as a couple on a genuine domestic basis when the deceased died. In that second case they must also have lived together in that way continuously for at least two years, or have a child together who was under 18 at the date of death.
- [medium] structure: These headings are labels. Two are built on colons, and 'framework' and 'angle' are a stock word and a casual one. The newer articles use the questions a reader types. 'The bottom line' is a stock phrase the checker flags.
  Current: Property: the same framework as marriage / Children of de facto relationships / Maintenance between former partners / Death without separation: the estate angle / Common de facto questions, answered / Protecting yourself, whichever side you are on / The bottom line
  Proposed: Is property divided the same way as for married couples? / Do the parenting and child support rules differ? / Can a former de facto partner claim maintenance? / What happens if a de facto partner dies? / Common questions / How can you protect yourself? (Remove The bottom line, and update the contents list and ids.)
- [medium] fees-and-links: The grandparents link has nothing to do with whether parents married, and it arrives as a clipped command. Meanwhile the child support guide, which the sentence before is about, is not linked. The parenting arrangements guide already covers grandparents.
  Current: Child support likewise runs through the same administrative assessment regardless of marital status. Read our guide to grandparents' rights in Victoria.
  Proposed: Child support also runs through the same administrative assessment whether or not the parents married, as our guide to how child support is calculated explains. (Link to /insight-child-support-australia and drop the grandparents sentence.)
- [medium] fees-and-links: The fee is right but leads nowhere useful. There is no link to the agreed settlement guide or the property settlement service page, the fees link opens the family law section rather than the fee, and 'a confidential first conversation' replaces the site's settled free first call wording.
  Current: Where you have already agreed how to divide your property, consent orders for an agreed property settlement are a fixed fee of $3,025 including GST, and our fees page sets out what that covers. Other work comes with a written fee estimate before it begins. Start at our family law page or call for a confidential first conversation.
  Proposed: Where you have already agreed how to divide your property, consent orders for an agreed property settlement are a fixed fee of $3,025 including GST, and our guide to making an agreed settlement final explains what that involves. Other work comes with a written fee estimate before any substantive work begins. Your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer. (Link consent orders to /service-property-settlement, the guide to /insight-agreed-property-settlement and the fee to /fees#fee-consent-orders-for-an-agreed-property-settlement.)
- [medium] voice: The closing summary repeats the lead and ends on a saying, and the final prompt ends on a slogan.
  Current: The law has caught up with modern relationships; make sure your paperwork has too. / Unsure whether your relationship qualifies, or racing a two-year deadline? Ask us this week, not next year.
  Proposed: Remove The bottom line heading and paragraph, and keep the separation checklist paragraph. Close with: Unsure whether your relationship qualifies, or close to the two-year deadline? Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
- [low] voice: 'Does not care' gives the law a personality, and it recurs in the children section. 'Framework' is a stock word, and the interrupted clause slows down the deadline. The lead is at the 70-word limit, so the fix keeps it shorter.
  Current: De facto partners in Victoria stand almost exactly where married couples stand at separation: property settlement, including superannuation splitting, runs on the same framework, and parenting law does not care whether you married. Two things differ. A property claim generally needs a gateway, such as two years together, a child or a registered relationship, and, without both partners' consent or the court's leave, must start within two years of separation.
  Proposed: De facto partners in Victoria stand almost exactly where married couples stand at separation: property settlement, including superannuation splitting, follows the same law, and so do parenting arrangements. Two things differ. A property claim generally needs a gateway, such as two years together, a child or a registered relationship, and must start within two years of separation unless both partners consent or the court gives leave. (66 words)
- [low] voice: These are clipped sayings that Spencer's rules exclude, and 'deed' is the wrong word for a Torrens title.
  Current: Title is not destiny. Property held in one partner's name still enters the pool; the questions are contribution and future need, not the name on the deed. / Keep clarity about big contributions: deposits from parents, renovations funded by one partner. Documents beat memories.
  Proposed: It still counts. Property held in one partner's name still forms part of the pool, and what matters is each person's contributions and future needs, not whose name is on the title. / Keep records of big contributions, such as a deposit from parents or renovations one partner paid for, because documents are far more reliable than memories.
- [low] legal-query: 'Routinely' states a typical court outcome with more certainty than the page can support, and readers may rely on it. Spencer should confirm he is comfortable with it, given the rule against promising outcomes. Not rewritten.
  Current: a two-and-a-half-year relationship with separate finances routinely produces a very modest adjustment, or none.
  Proposed: For Spencer's view. If he wants it softened, 'can produce' in place of 'routinely produces' would do it.

## insight-divorce-in-victoria.html
Scores: voice 6, clarity 8, persuasion 7, answer first 6

Accurate, well cross-linked and sensibly frank ('Plenty of people file their own divorce'), with the fixed fees quoted correctly and a mid-article prompt straight after the property deadline. It loses marks on answer-first. The first section opens with history, the short marriage section with the old rule, and the cost section with the court's fee rather than the firm's. There are staged openings and a 'bottom line' summary, and one line about deadlines reads as a guarantee. The body says 'twelve' ten times, against '12' in its own headline and title and on the divorce service page.

- [medium] answer-first: The heading asks how long, and the answer arrives only in the second sentence. 'Fault' sits in scare quotes, and the Act is tacked on as a separate last sentence of the section.
  Current: Australia has had no-fault divorce for fifty years. The Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia grants a divorce when satisfied the marriage has broken down irretrievably, demonstrated by the parties having lived separately and apart for at least twelve months, with no reasonable likelihood of reconciliation. Whose "fault" the separation was is legally irrelevant. ... Divorce is granted under the Family Law Act 1975 (Cth).
  Proposed: You need to have been separated for at least 12 months. Under the Family Law Act 1975 (Cth), the Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia grants a divorce when it is satisfied that the marriage has broken down irretrievably, which is shown by the parties having lived separately and apart for at least 12 months with no reasonable likelihood of reconciliation. Australia has had no-fault divorce for 50 years, so whose fault the separation was is legally irrelevant. (Keep the two reconciliation sentences and delete the last sentence.)
- [medium] answer-first: The section opens with history and ends on a commentary tail. The yes is only implied, in the third sentence.
  Current: For decades, couples married less than two years first had to attend counselling and file a certificate before applying. That requirement was repealed with effect from June 2025. Short marriages now divorce on the same twelve-month separation test as everyone else, one of several recent changes that have quietly modernised the system.
  Proposed: Yes, on the same 12-month separation test as everyone else. Couples married less than two years used to have to attend counselling and file a certificate before applying, but that requirement was repealed with effect from June 2025.
- [medium] fees-and-links: The section opens with the court's fee rather than answering what the reader asked. The fees link opens the top of the fees page instead of the divorce fees or the divorce service page, which sets out what each fee covers.
  Current: A court filing fee applies, with a substantially reduced fee for concession card holders and those in financial hardship. Our own fee is fixed at $1,089 for a joint application and $1,452 for a sole application, including GST, and our fixed fees page sets out what each covers. ... If a wedding is planned, remember celebrants need notice, so sequence the dates carefully.
  Proposed: Our fixed fee is $1,089 for a joint application and $1,452 for a sole application, including GST, and our divorce page sets out what each covers. The court's filing fee is separate and paid directly to the court, with a substantially reduced fee for concession card holders and those in financial hardship. From filing, a straightforward divorce is typically listed within a few months, and the divorce order then takes effect one month and one day after it is made. Only then are you free to remarry, so if a wedding is planned, allow for the notice a celebrant needs when you set the dates. (Link the divorce page to /service-divorce and the fees to /fees#fee-divorce-application-joint.)
- [medium] voice: A staged opening builds up to the point, and the next-but-one sentence opens with 'And'. The heading's yes never comes first. The agreed settlement guide, the natural next step for the reader the following callout addresses, is not linked.
  Current: Here is the point most people miss. Divorce ends the marriage; it does not divide property or fix spousal maintenance. And once the divorce takes effect, you generally have just twelve months to commence property settlement or maintenance proceedings. ... Our guides to property after separation and to spousal maintenance in Victoria explain both processes.
  Proposed: Yes. Divorce ends the marriage, but it does not divide property or fix spousal maintenance, and once the divorce takes effect you generally have just 12 months to start property settlement or maintenance proceedings. ... Our guides to property after separation, to making an agreed settlement final and to spousal maintenance in Victoria explain each process. (Link to /insight-agreed-property-settlement.)
- [medium] voice: 'We make sure ... nothing is lost to a deadline' reads as a guarantee, outside the three service promises. Two list items are garbled ('the marriage certificate is overseas', 'any disagreement about arrangements'), and the section ends on a fragment.
  Current: Involve a lawyer where service will be difficult, the marriage certificate is overseas or not in English, separation under one roof must be proved, there are children and any disagreement about arrangements, or property remains unresolved. At Spencer Alexander Lawyers we handle divorce applications on the fixed fees set out above, and we make sure the divorce is sequenced with property and parenting so nothing is lost to a deadline. Start at our family law page.
  Proposed: A lawyer is worth involving where service will be difficult, you married overseas or the marriage certificate is not in English, separation under one roof must be proved, there are children and you disagree about their arrangements, or property remains unresolved. We handle divorce applications on the fixed fees set out above, and we advise on when to file so that the divorce does not start the 12-month property deadline before you are ready. (Keep the /service-divorce link on 'divorce applications'.)
- [medium] voice: The checker flags the stock heading. The section summarises what the reader has just read and closes on a saying, and the final prompt uses a slogan where the site's free call wording belongs.
  Current: What is the bottom line on divorce in Victoria? ... Handled in the right order, the legal side of separation is smaller than it looks from the inside. / Thinking about separation, or already served with an application? One conversation maps your next steps.
  Proposed: Remove the heading, its paragraph and its contents entry, and keep the checklist paragraph. Close with: Thinking about separation, or already served with an application? Your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer. Call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.
- [low] voice: This uses the 'not X; it is Y' structure, opens a sentence with 'But', and uses the casual 'sloppy'.
  Current: This is not a parenting trial; it is a check. But it is why the application asks detailed questions about the children, and why sloppy answers cause adjournments.
  Proposed: The court checks that proper arrangements exist rather than deciding what they should be, which is why the application asks detailed questions about the children and why incomplete answers can lead to an adjournment.
- [low] consistency: The body says 'twelve' ten times, while the headline, title, description and the divorce service page say '12', as the voice rules require from 10 upwards. 'Decree' appears once where the article otherwise says divorce order. This page says the order takes effect 'one month and one day' after it is made, while service-divorce.html says 'one month'. Section 55(1)(a) reads 'at the expiration of a period of 1 month from the making of the order', so Spencer should pick one form for both pages. The overseas marriage answer mixes 'you or your spouse' with 'your permanent home', and it paraphrases domicile differently from the service page.
  Current: twelve months / not after the decree / you or your spouse is an Australian citizen, treats Australia as your permanent home, or has ordinarily lived in Australia for the twelve months before filing.
  Proposed: Write '12 months' throughout and 'after the divorce order'. For the FAQ, use the service page's wording: Usually, yes. The court can grant a divorce if, on the day the application is filed, either of you is an Australian citizen, is domiciled in Australia, which broadly means living here and intending to make your home here indefinitely, or has ordinarily lived in Australia for the 12 months before that day. (Mirror any change in the FAQPage schema.)
- [low] voice: The lead ends with a signpost that adds nothing and uses 11 of the lead's 70 words.
  Current: Here is how it works, and the traps we help clients avoid.
  Proposed: Delete it, and write '12 months' for both figures in the lead.

## insight-enforcing-property-orders.html
Scores: voice 8, clarity 8, persuasion 8, answer first 9

The newest and best written of the six. The lead answers the headline in five words and names every remedy, each section answers its question, the call prompt sits where readers realise they need help, and costs are dealt with frankly. What remains is mostly polish: two staged openings, an awkward tense, a passive sentence where the firm should be the subject, and a dangling sentence on maintenance. There is also one legal query: the National Enforcement List details still rest on secondary sources, and the court's site is still unreachable today. The checker's one hard failure is a false positive.

- [medium] legal-query: DECISIONS.md records that fcfcoa.gov.au returned 403 when this article was written on 19 Oct 2026. The start date, scope, first return hearing and costs warning were therefore taken from search results and the Queensland Law Society's Proctor rather than from the practice direction itself. The site still refused the connection when I tried today. The accuracy routine should read the practice direction once the site can be reached. Not rewritten.
  Current: From 21 September 2026, the Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia has sent enforcement applications in financial and property matters to a dedicated National Enforcement List, under a practice direction made for that purpose. ... Applications are listed for a first return hearing unless an urgent hearing has been granted, and the practice direction warns that a respondent who makes enforcement necessary may be ordered to pay the applicant's legal costs.
  Proposed: No substantive rewrite until verified. On tense alone: Since 21 September 2026, the Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia has sent enforcement applications in financial and property matters to a dedicated National Enforcement List, under a practice direction made for that purpose.
- [low] answer-first: The first sentence points at a section instead of answering, and the answer only arrives in the next sentence. 'So the transfer can be completed without your former partner's cooperation.' then repeats it.
  Current: Section 106A of the Act deals with this directly. Where an order directs a person to sign a deed or instrument and they refuse or neglect to do so, the court can appoint an officer of the court or another person to sign it in their name and to do whatever else is needed to make it effective.
  Proposed: The court can appoint someone to sign it for them. Under section 106A of the Act, where an order directs a person to sign a deed or instrument and they refuse or neglect to do so, the court can appoint an officer of the court or another person to sign it in their name and to do whatever else is needed to make it effective. (Delete 'So the transfer can be completed without your former partner's cooperation.' and start the next sentence 'If they have instead tried to sell...'.)
- [low] voice: The sentence builds up to its point instead of stating it.
  Current: What the court will not do is enforce something the orders do not say.
  Proposed: The court enforces only what the orders say.
- [low] voice: The passive leaves unclear who weighs the cost. The firm should be the subject, which also reads as advice the reader can rely on.
  Current: None of that guarantees you will recover everything you spend, so the cost of enforcing is weighed against what is owed before an application is filed.
  Proposed: None of that guarantees you will recover everything you spend, so we weigh the cost of enforcing against what is owed before any application is filed.
- [low] clarity: The sentence tells the reader there is a difference without saying what it is or whether it affects them. For Spencer: cut it, or explain it in one sentence once checked against the rules.
  Current: Periodic maintenance payments are treated differently.
  Proposed: Delete the sentence, or replace it with one verified sentence saying how interest applies to periodic maintenance.
- [low] checker: check_style.py fails the page for a bracket in prose, but '(Family Law)' is part of the instrument's official title. The checker only exempts bracketed title words that come before 'Act'. The page needs no change. Extend the checker's exemption to Rules and Regulations so the failure stops recurring.
  Current: Federal Circuit and Family Court of Australia (Family Law) Rules 2021 (Cth), Part 11.1.
  Proposed: No change to the page.

## insight-grandparents-rights-victoria.html
Scores: voice 7, clarity 7, persuasion 6, answer first 8

The closest of the six to Spencer's voice. It answers the question in its first line, flows rather than clips, stays careful about the law, puts its call prompt where a worried grandparent will feel the need for help, and gives practical steps a reader can act on. What holds it back is weight in the wrong places: an 80 word sentence in the section readers most want, a verbless 60 word opening to the orders section, and a vague comparison in the bereavement section. It also closes on a generic hub link with no fixed fee, although the parenting service page acts for grandparents and fees.html prices consent orders.

- [medium] clarity: This is the section a worried grandparent most wants, and it opens with a verbless fragment, then runs five considerations into one 80 word sentence, the longest on these six pages. A reader has to go back to see where one factor ends and the next begins. The proposal keeps every consideration and every qualifier, split into two sentences.
  Current: The child's best interests, above everything else. In practice that usually means the court considers the existing relationship between the grandparent and the child and what continuing or losing it would mean for the child, the child's own wishes where their age and maturity make that appropriate, the views and reasons of the parents for restricting contact, the practical workability of any arrangement, and any risk to the child's safety, including from family violence or conflict that spending time with the grandparent would expose the child to.
  Proposed: A court looks at the child's best interests, above everything else. In practice that usually means it considers the existing relationship between the grandparent and the child, and what continuing or losing it would mean for the child, along with the child's own wishes where their age and maturity make that appropriate. It also weighs the views of the parents and their reasons for restricting contact, whether any arrangement would work in practice, and any risk to the child's safety, including from family violence or from conflict that spending time with the grandparent would expose the child to.
- [low] voice: The section opens with a 60 word fragment that has no main verb. It closes with 'This is why', which links the negotiated outcome to the sentence before it about the parents' primacy, but the reason actually given after the colon is that agreed arrangements serve the child better. Both passages read like notes rather than explanation.
  Current: The same range of parenting orders available to any person concerned with a child's care, welfare or development: orders for the child to spend time with the grandparent, whether regularly, during holidays, or by other communication such as phone or video calls, and in less common cases orders addressing where a child lives or who makes decisions about their care. ... This is why most grandparent matters are resolved through negotiation or mediation rather than a final court order: an agreed arrangement that keeps the relationship going tends to serve the child better than a hard fought result imposed by a judge.
  Proposed: Grandparents can ask for the same range of parenting orders as any other person concerned with a child's care, welfare or development. That includes orders for the child to spend time with the grandparent, whether regularly, during holidays or through other communication such as phone or video calls, and in less common cases orders about where a child lives or who makes decisions about their care. ... Most grandparent matters are resolved through negotiation or mediation rather than a final court order, and for good reason: an agreed arrangement that keeps the relationship going tends to serve the child better than a hard fought result imposed by a judge.
- [low] persuasion: The page ends on a generic link to the top of the hub and names no price. Yet service-parenting-arrangements.html says the firm acts for grandparents, and fees.html prices consent orders for agreed parenting arrangements at $3,025 including GST. A grandparent who reaches agreement with the parents should see what it costs to make that binding. Spencer should confirm the fixed fee applies where a grandparent is a party: fees.html describes it as covering 'the arrangements you have agreed' and assumes no family violence or child safety concerns.
  Current: If contact with a grandchild has broken down and informal efforts have not worked, early advice on your options is worth more than time spent waiting for things to resolve themselves. Learn more about our family law practice.
  Proposed: If contact with a grandchild has broken down and informal efforts have not worked, early advice on your options is worth more than time spent waiting for things to resolve themselves. Where you and the parents reach agreement, consent orders recording it are a fixed fee of $3,025 including GST, and our fees page sets out what that covers. Our parenting arrangements page explains how we help grandparents as well as parents. [Link 'our fees page' to /fees#family-law and 'Our parenting arrangements page' to /service-parenting-arrangements.]
- [low] clarity: 'Higher' has nothing to compare against, so the reader cannot tell which situation the warning is about. The rest of the paragraph shows the point is the period after a parent's death.
  Current: The pathway is the same whether the rupture came from a separation, a family falling out, or a parent's death, though the practical stakes can be higher.
  Proposed: The pathway is the same whether the rupture came from a separation, a family falling out or a parent's death, though the stakes in practice are often higher after a death.
- [low] headings: This is the only body heading that is a label rather than a question. The question form matches how grandparents search and how the other headings on the page read. Change the In this article entry to match and keep the existing id.
  Current: Practical steps if you are being kept from your grandchildren
  Proposed: What should you do if you are being kept from your grandchildren?
- [low] legal-query: 'Broader options for formalising that care' is too vague to help the grandparent most likely to call: one already caring for a child day to day. A reader cannot tell whether it means further Family Law Act orders or a Victorian process outside family law. This is a statement about the law, so it is not rewritten here.
  Current: Depending on the circumstances, that can include a parenting order addressing where the child lives or who they spend time with, and in some cases a grandparent already caring for a child day to day may need to consider broader options for formalising that care.
  Proposed: For Spencer: name the options he has in mind, once read in the authorised legislation, or leave the sentence as it stands. No wording proposed.

## insight-intervention-orders.html
Scores: voice 5, clarity 6, persuasion 7, answer first 6

Strong on urgency and on the respondent's position, with a mid article prompt placed exactly where a respondent realises they need help. But the voice is the most clipped of the six. Lines such as 'that instinct produces breach charges daily', 'Unilaterally ignoring it is a charge' and 'act in days, not weeks; put evidence before emotion' read as advertising copy rather than Spencer's measured explanation. Most headings are labels, one of them misleads a protected person, the safety section names no support line, and the closing help section offers fixed appearance fees that the fees page does not.

- [medium] firm-facts: 'Fixed appearances quoted where possible' conflicts with fees.html, which lists no intervention order fee and says a dispute, or anything outside a listed fee, is charged on a written estimate. The family law hub says the same. The phrase is also jargon a frightened reader will not understand. It is the same kind of mismatch DECISIONS.md flagged for the business structures article on 20 Oct 2026. 'Complete discretion always' reads as puffery, and the link goes to the top of the hub rather than its family violence section. The proposal uses only the confirmed promise of after hours contact for urgent matters and the written estimate rule.
  Current: Tell us the matter is urgent and it is treated that way from the first call. We act for applicants and respondents across Melbourne's Magistrates' Courts, with fixed appearances quoted where possible and complete discretion always. Our family law page explains how we work.
  Proposed: Tell us the matter is urgent and it is treated that way from the first call, and after hours contact is available for urgent matters. We act for applicants and respondents in the Magistrates' Courts across Melbourne, always in confidence, and because every intervention order matter is different, your fee is set out in a written estimate before any substantive work begins. Our family law page explains how we help with family violence and intervention orders. [Link to /family-law#svc-family-violence-and-intervention-orders.]
- [medium] headings: 'Breaches are criminal in both directions' suggests a protected person can commit a breach, which the paragraph beneath it rules out. A frightened applicant may read it as a warning not to reply to a message. Five other headings are labels where the rest of the site uses questions. 'What should you do if an order is made against you?' sits over a section about being served with an application, not an order. 'The bottom line' is a stock phrase the checker flags. Keep the existing ids and change the In this article list to match.
  Current: Breaches are criminal in both directions | Interim orders: protection before proof | What conditions can say | If you need protection | What should you do if an order is made against you? | How intervention orders meet family law | Discreet, immediate help | The bottom line
  Proposed: What happens if an order is breached? | Can an order be made before the respondent is heard? | What conditions can an order include? | What should you do if you need protection? | What should you do if you are served with an application? | How does an intervention order affect a family law case? | How we help | What matters most in the first days?
- [medium] voice: 'Shapes everything' overstates. 'Produces breach charges daily' is an unsupported figure dressed as a slogan. The advice is right, but the reader gets it as a headline rather than with the reason behind it.
  Current: Being served with an application does not make you guilty of anything, but how you respond in the first fortnight shapes everything. Do not contact the applicant about the case: that instinct produces breach charges daily.
  Proposed: Being served with an application does not make you guilty of anything, but what you do in the first fortnight shapes the options you have at court. Do not contact the applicant about the case, however natural that feels, because a single message can lead to a breach charge where an order is in place.
- [medium] clarity: These endings are clipped and also loose. Ignoring an order or sending a text is a breach that can lead to a charge; it is not itself 'a charge'. The first pair sits in an FAQ answer, so the FAQPage schema must change with it.
  Current: An order that no longer fits the family's reality is a problem the court can fix. Unilaterally ignoring it is a charge. [and in the breach section] A text that says only "please talk to me" can be a charge.
  Proposed: If an order no longer fits the family's circumstances, the answer is to ask the court to change it, because ignoring it can lead to a criminal charge. [breach section] Even a text that says only "please talk to me" can lead to a charge.
- [low] answer-first: The answer to a yes or no question never actually says no, and it runs as three clipped fragments. 'Touch employment' is vague, and 'negotiation rather than resignation' is a flourish. Mirror the change in the FAQPage schema.
