## resource-separation-first-30-days.html
Scores: voice 7, clarity 8, persuasion 5, answer first 6

This checklist is warm, practical and accurate, with plain step headings and careful legal statements. Three things weaken it. The lead opens on a rhetorical line, and at 76 words it is longer than the article lead limit. The deadlines section has no call prompt. The closing 'How we help' paragraph is generic: it never names the free first call, a fixed fee or a family service page.

- [medium] answer-first: The lead opens with a rhetorical hook and only says what the page offers in its third sentence. At 76 words it is longer than the 70 word lead the gate enforces for articles, and 'thirty' clashes with the title's '30'.
  Current: Separation rarely arrives with a to-do list. In the first days and weeks most people are coping with an upheaval while being asked to make decisions about money, the children and the future, often without knowing what needs attention now and what can wait. This checklist sets out the steps that matter in the first thirty days after separating in Victoria, in a sensible order, and none of them requires you to have decided anything final.
  Proposed: This checklist sets out the steps that matter in the first 30 days after separating in Victoria, in a sensible order, and none of them requires you to have decided anything final. In those weeks most people are coping with an upheaval while being asked to make decisions about money, the children and the future, often without knowing what needs attention now and what can wait.
- [medium] call-prompt: Section 6 states the 12 month and two year limits, but the page's only mid prompt comes a section earlier and is about wills. A reader working out their own deadline has nothing to act on at the point where it matters most. The site's rule for articles is a prompt straight after the section that states a time limit.
  Current: Our guides to divorce in Victoria and property settlement after separation explain both deadlines in context.
  Proposed: Keep the sentence and add a prompt paragraph after it in the existing style: 'If you are not sure which date applies to you, or it is close, call (03) 9125 8355 or send an enquiry.' Link the number with tel:+61391258355 and the enquiry with /contact#enquiry. Change 'twelve months' in this section to '12 months'.
- [medium] persuasion: This is the page's closing case for calling, and it is its weakest paragraph. It relies on a from-through structure and a group of three, then ends on 'an initial conversation is a good place to start'. It does not say the first call is free, it names none of the fixed fees a separating couple will need, and it links none of service-property-settlement, service-parenting-arrangements or service-divorce.
  Current: Spencer Alexander Lawyers advises clients across Melbourne and Victoria from the earliest days of a separation through to a finalised property settlement and parenting arrangement. If you want to talk through where you stand, what needs attention now, and what can wait, an initial conversation is a good place to start.
  Proposed: We act for clients across Melbourne and Victoria from the first days of a separation until the property settlement and parenting arrangements are final. Your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer, who can tell you what needs attention now and what can wait. Once you have agreed terms, our fixed fee for consent orders is $3,025 including GST, for a property settlement or for parenting arrangements, and a joint divorce application is $1,089. Link the three service pages and the fee anchors.
- [low] persuasion: This prompt sits beside the one step that has a published price, but it does not give the price. It also says 'your ex' where the body says 'former partner', so the same person has two names on one page.
  Current: Worried your ex is still named in your will, your power of attorney or your super nomination? A short call can confirm what needs updating and what does not.
  Proposed: Worried your former partner is still named in your will, your power of attorney or your super nomination? A short call can confirm what needs updating and what does not, and our fixed fee is $495 including GST for a new single will and $495 for an enduring power of attorney. Make the same change from 'your ex' to 'your former partner' in step 5.
- [low] voice: The heading is a clever phrase where Spencer's headings are plain statements. The sentence uses a staged opening ('What to avoid is') and the informal 'maxing out'.
  Current: Heading '2. Separate your immediate finances, practically rather than punitively' and 'What to avoid is emptying a joint account or maxing out a shared credit card as a tactic: it rarely helps your position and can complicate the eventual settlement.'
  Proposed: Heading '2. Separate your everyday finances' and 'Do not empty a joint account or run up a shared credit card as a tactic, because it rarely helps your position and can complicate the eventual settlement.'
- [low] voice: The paragraph sets up an assumption only to knock it down ('Neither is true') and ends on a tidy summary line. 'Actual', 'specific' and 'simply' add nothing.
  Current: A common misconception is that seeing a lawyer means the separation is now a fight, or that you need to already know what you want. Neither is true. An early conversation is where you find out what your actual options are, what the realistic range of outcomes looks like, and which of the steps above matter most in your specific situation. Getting advice early does not commit you to court, or to anything at all. It simply means the decisions you make in the following weeks and months are informed ones.
  Proposed: Seeing a lawyer early does not turn a separation into a fight, and you do not need to know what you want before you call. An early conversation tells you what your options are, the realistic range of outcomes and which of the steps above matter most in your situation, and it does not commit you to court or to anything else, so the decisions you make in the following weeks and months are informed ones.
- [low] clarity: 'That appointment' fits the executor but not a gift, so a reader who has left their partner a gift may think only the executorship survives separation. The legal meaning is unchanged in the proposal.
  Current: If your former partner is named as a beneficiary or executor in your will, that appointment generally remains fully valid until you change it.
  Proposed: If your will leaves anything to your former partner or names them as executor, the gift and the appointment generally remain fully valid until you change the will.
- [low] voice: The second sentence has to be read twice, because 'the basic picture is not a scramble to reconstruct from memory' is a muddled image.
  Current: You are not building a case against anyone by doing this. You are making sure that whenever advice or negotiation does happen, the basic picture is not a scramble to reconstruct from memory.
  Proposed: Making copies is not building a case against anyone. It means that when you get advice or start negotiating, you can work from the records rather than trying to reconstruct them from memory.

## resource-executor-first-steps.html
Scores: voice 8, clarity 7, persuasion 7, answer first 6

This is the strongest page in the set: concrete, calm and in Spencer's register, with a mid prompt and a named fee. The lead circles before it answers, and it says 'nothing is urgent' three times in its first 100 words. One 75 word sentence on commission needs splitting. The fee and the no will answer should link to fees.html and the probate page and give the prices fees.html publishes.

- [medium] answer-first: Two framing sentences come before the page says what it gives, and the second is a clipped list of four items. 'The law is rarely the urgent part' is then repeated in the step 1 heading and in step 1's first sentence.
  Current: When a will names you as executor, the law is rarely the urgent part. The first weeks are practical: the funeral, the death certificate, the house, the paperwork. This checklist sets out those first steps in a sensible order, what can proceed straight away, what must wait until probate is granted, and the records worth starting on day one, before you have decided anything about the estate.
  Proposed: If a will names you as executor, very little is legally urgent in the first weeks, and most of the work is practical: arranging the funeral, getting the death certificate, securing the house and starting the paperwork. This checklist sets out those steps in a sensible order, including what can proceed straight away, what must wait until probate is granted and the records worth keeping from day one.
- [medium] clarity: At 75 words, two separate conditions run together, and 'or you are not entitled' comes so late that a reader cannot tell which condition it attaches to. The split below keeps the legal meaning: the consequence stays attached to the notice duty, as it is now.
  Current: If the will was signed on or after 1 November 2017, a payment clause counts only if the will maker gave written informed consent to it before signing, and you must tell each beneficiary whose share would be reduced, as soon as reasonably practicable, the basis of the payment, how it is calculated, its estimated amount and their right to ask the court to review it, or you are not entitled to commission or fees.
  Proposed: If the will was signed on or after 1 November 2017, a payment clause counts only if the will maker gave written informed consent to it before signing. You must also tell each beneficiary whose share would be reduced, as soon as reasonably practicable, the basis of the payment, how it is calculated, its estimated amount and their right to ask the court to review it, or you are not entitled to commission or fees.
- [medium] persuasion: The price is given but not linked to fees.html#fee-probate-application or to service-probate, so the reader cannot see what 'straightforward' means. The no will answer leaves out the published $2,178 fee for letters of administration. CLAUDE.md requires that fee to be described as for where there is no will.
  Current: 'Our fixed fee for a probate application for a straightforward estate is $1,815 including GST.' and 'If no will can be found, the estate is distributed under Victoria's intestacy rules and someone, usually the closest next of kin, applies for letters of administration instead of probate. Most of this checklist still applies while that is sorted out.'
  Proposed: Our fixed fee for a probate application in a straightforward estate is $1,815 including GST, and our fees page sets out what counts as straightforward. / Most of this checklist still applies while that is sorted out, and our fixed fee for letters of administration in a straightforward estate where there is no will is $2,178 including GST. Link 'probate application' to /service-probate and each fee to its /fees#fee- anchor.
- [low] legal-query: The reader is not told what the 5 per cent is measured against, so the cap cannot be applied to an estate. The substance is not rewritten here.
  Current: Payment for the executor's time and trouble, called commission, is sometimes available, generally where the will provides for it or where the beneficiaries or the court approve it, and the court cannot allow more than 5 per cent.
  Proposed: No change to the substance until it is checked. Confirm in the commission provision of the Administration and Probate Act 1958 what the 5 per cent is calculated on, and say so in plain words in the same sentence.
- [low] voice: 'Take a breath:' is a clever heading where a plain one is needed. The first sentence repeats both the heading and the lead.
  Current: Heading '1. Take a breath: very little is legally urgent' and 'Almost nothing in an estate has to happen in the first days. The funeral needs arranging and the household needs securing; the rest of the estate will keep for a week or two while you grieve and gather yourself.'
  Proposed: Heading '1. Very little is legally urgent in the first days' and body opening 'The funeral needs arranging and the household needs securing, but the rest of the estate will keep for a week or two while you grieve and gather yourself.'
- [low] voice: The paragraph stages its point ('Two things... The first... The second...') instead of saying it.
  Current: Two things catch executors here. The first is insurance: check that home, contents and vehicle policies are current, and tell the insurer if a house is now unoccupied, because an empty home can affect cover. The second is the clean out that happens too early. Well meaning relatives clearing the house in the first fortnight is one of the most common sources of estate disputes, so nothing should leave the house yet, however certain everyone feels about who was meant to have what.
  Proposed: Check that home, contents and vehicle policies are current, and tell the insurer if a house is now unoccupied, because an empty home can affect cover. Do not let anything leave the house yet, however certain everyone feels about who was meant to have what, because well meaning relatives clearing a house in the first fortnight is one of the most common sources of estate disputes.

## privacy.html
Scores: voice 6, clarity 7, persuasion 6, answer first 7

The policy is accurate about the site's real practices: no analytics, self hosted fonts and FormSubmit delivery. But it opens on a cliché, and it says it is dated 5 September 2026 although it changed on 28 September. It also fails the style checker on one sentence and lists what it collects in a 62 word sentence with semicolons where prose would read better.

- [medium] factual: The policy's substance changed on 28 Sep 2026, when commit b7434cf replaced the Google Fonts paragraph, and the sitemap lastmod is 2026-09-28. The page still says this version is dated 5 September 2026, and two adjacent paragraphs give two different date statements.
  Current: 'We may update this policy from time to time. The current version is always the one on this page, and this version is dated 5 September 2026.' and 'This policy was last reviewed in September 2026. It is general information about our practices, not legal advice about your situation.'
  Proposed: We may update this policy from time to time, and the current version is always the one on this page. This version is dated 28 September 2026, and it is general information about our practices, not legal advice about your situation.
- [medium] style-checker: check_style.py fails this sentence as calling the firm an expert. The proposal clears the failure and says more precisely who is meant.
  Current: We disclose it only where you consent, where the law requires or permits it, or as needed to provide our services, for example to a barrister, expert or other professional engaged for your matter with your knowledge.
  Proposed: We disclose it only where you consent, where the law requires or permits it, or as needed to provide our services. For example, a barrister, an expert witness or another professional engaged for your matter may receive it, with your knowledge. The checker passes this, with a note only.
- [low] voice: 'Takes ... seriously' is a stock line, and 'referred to as we, us or the firm' is drafting language in a page written for ordinary readers.
  Current: Spencer Alexander Lawyers, referred to as we, us or the firm, takes the privacy of the people we deal with seriously. This policy explains how we handle personal information in line with the Australian Privacy Principles in the Privacy Act 1988 (Cth).
  Proposed: This policy explains how Spencer Alexander Lawyers handles personal information in line with the Australian Privacy Principles in the Privacy Act 1988 (Cth). The sentence that follows stays as it is.
- [low] clarity: A colon after 'includes' and a 62 word list held together by semicolons makes this hard to read. It also does not mention the form's newer questions, the best time for a call back and whether it is safe to email or leave a message, which can themselves be sensitive.
  Current: That includes: your name, contact details and the content of your message when you use the enquiry form on this website or email or call us; your email address if you ask to receive a resource or updates from us; and the information you provide if you engage us to act for you, which can include sensitive information relevant to your matter.
  Proposed: When you use the enquiry form on this website, email us or call us, we collect your name, your contact details, your message and your other answers on the form, such as the best time to call you and whether it is safe to email you or leave a message. If you ask to receive a resource or updates from us, we collect your email address, and if you engage us to act for you, we collect the information you provide, which can include sensitive information relevant to your matter.
- [low] legal-query: The OAIC can deal only with complaints about entities bound by the Australian Privacy Principles. A small business is generally exempt unless its annual turnover exceeds $3 million or it has chosen to be treated as bound, though anti-money laundering obligations, which reach many law practices from 1 July 2026, may bring it in for those activities. The 'What we collect' section also does not mention identity documents gathered to verify clients. The substance is not rewritten here.
  Current: If you are not satisfied with our response, you can raise your concern with the Office of the Australian Information Commissioner at oaic.gov.au or, for matters concerning our conduct as a law practice, with the Victorian Legal Services Board and Commissioner at lsbc.vic.gov.au.
  Proposed: No change to the substance until it is checked. Confirm the basis on which the Privacy Act applies to the firm, then adjust this sentence and the collection list to match. Link oaic.gov.au and lsbc.vic.gov.au, which are plain text at present.

## service-divorce.html
Scores: voice 8, clarity 8, persuasion 7, answer first 8

Strong, specific and accurate; it reads like a lawyer explaining the process across a desk, and its closing line is the model for the other seven. The cost answer leads with the court's fee instead of our fixed fee, the first screen gives no price although the page has two fixed fees, and the body never links the property settlement or wills pages it tells the reader they will need. A few sentences in the steps and the sole application block need a second read.

- [medium] answer-first: The question is how much a divorce costs, and the answer opens with a court fee it does not state, so the firm's fixed fee, the figure the reader came for, arrives second. The same answer on faq.html has the same order and should change with it.
  Current: The Court charges a filing fee, and a reduced fee is available to concession card holders and people in financial hardship, although for a joint application both of you must qualify. Our own fee is fixed at $1,089 for a joint application and $1,452 for a sole one, including GST, and our fees page sets out what each covers.
  Proposed: Our fixed fee is $1,089 for a joint application and $1,452 for a sole one, including GST, and our fees page sets out what each covers. The Court also charges a filing fee, and a reduced fee is available to concession card holders and people in financial hardship, although for a joint application both of you must qualify.
- [medium] links: The lead, this block and the time limits section all tell the reader a divorce leads to a property settlement and a will review, but nothing in the body links the property settlement or wills service pages; the only route is the generic hub link at the foot of related reading. Words unchanged, links added.
  Current: A divorce taking effect starts the time for property and maintenance claims, so we advise on whether to settle property first. It also cancels any gift in your will to your former spouse and their appointment as executor, unless it appears you wanted them to stand, so we review your will too.
  Proposed: A divorce taking effect starts the time for property and maintenance claims, so we advise on whether to [settle property first](/service-property-settlement). It also cancels any gift in your will to your former spouse and their appointment as executor, unless it appears you wanted them to stand, so we [review your will too](/service-wills-and-estate-planning).
- [low] clarity: The word 'so' does not carry the will: the reason given is that a divorce leaves property and parenting undecided, yet the conclusion adds the will, whose connection to divorce the page explains only further down. The rewrite keeps the lead at 64 words.
  Current: A divorce needs 12 months of separation, and it does not divide property or decide parenting, so we also advise on your property settlement and your will.
  Proposed: A divorce needs 12 months of separation. It does not divide property or decide parenting, and it can change how your will operates, so we also advise on your property settlement and your will.
- [low] clarity: A 40 word sentence that opens on two dangling conditions, 'If asked, and once satisfied', before the reader knows who asks or what is being decided; it needs a second read. The rewrite keeps every condition, in the order the FAQ on the same page uses.
  Current: If asked, and once satisfied that proper arrangements have been made for any children of the marriage under 18, the Court can decide the application without anyone attending, unless the other spouse asks to attend or the Court directs otherwise. The order generally takes effect one month after it is made.
  Proposed: If you ask it to, the Court can decide the application without anyone attending, unless the other spouse asks to attend or the Court directs otherwise. Where there are children of the marriage under 18, it must first be satisfied that proper arrangements have been made for them. The order generally takes effect one month after it is made.
- [low] clarity: 'By hand from anyone but you' reads as though the papers come from someone, and 'acknowledgment' is spelt differently from the business sale page's 'acknowledgement'.
  Current: Either spouse can apply alone, and the application must then be served on the other, by post with an acknowledgment form or by hand from anyone but you.
  Proposed: Either spouse can apply alone, and the application must then be served on the other spouse, by post with an acknowledgement form or by hand by someone other than you.
- [low] call-prompt: It states a 28 day deadline and stops, with no prompt, although a spouse who has just been served is the reader most likely to be up against time.
  Current: We also act for spouses served with a divorce application. A response disputing it, for example over the date you separated, must generally be filed and served within 28 days after service, although the Court can extend that time.
  Proposed: We also act for spouses served with a divorce application. A response disputing it, for example over the date you separated, must generally be filed and served within 28 days after service, although the Court can extend that time. If you have been served and do not agree with the application, call us as soon as you can.

## service-property-settlement.html
Scores: voice 7, clarity 7, persuasion 7, answer first 8

Thorough and careful, with the clearest explanation on the site of why a private agreement has to be made binding. The time limits section, the most consequential part of the page, ends without a prompt to call, two sentences stack so many clauses that they need rereading, and the first screen names a fixed fee without its price.

- [medium] call-prompt: The section that states the page's most consequential deadline ends without telling the reader to call, where the divorce page ends its equivalent section with 'call us now', and its 52 word sentence carries both the rule and the leave test. Split, with the legal wording unchanged, and close with the prompt.
  Current: After that, a claim needs both people's consent or the court's leave, and the court grants leave for a property claim only if satisfied that hardship would otherwise be caused, to either spouse or a child after a marriage, or to the person applying or a child after a de facto relationship. Filing before the deadline means you need neither.
  Proposed: After that, a claim needs both people's consent or the court's leave. The court grants leave for a property claim only if it is satisfied that hardship would otherwise be caused, to either spouse or a child after a marriage, or to the person applying or a child after a de facto relationship. Filing before the deadline means you need neither. If your divorce has taken effect or your de facto relationship has ended, and nothing has been filed or formalised, call us now.
- [medium] clarity: The second limb, 'and of any material waste', hangs from 'the effect' many words earlier, so the sentence has to be read twice to see that the Act lists two separate matters.
  Current: Since the 2025 reforms, the Act expressly lists the effect of any family violence on what a person could contribute and on their circumstances, and of any material waste of property or financial resources caused intentionally or recklessly.
  Proposed: Since the 2025 reforms, the Act expressly lists the effect of any family violence on what a person could contribute and on their circumstances. It also lists the effect of any material waste of property or financial resources caused intentionally or recklessly.
- [low] answer-first: The question asks what can be done, and the answer opens by restating the duty in the same words as the Disclosure and negotiation block above it; the remedy, which is the answer, comes second.
  Current: Each of you owes the other full and frank disclosure while preparing for a property case, and the court as well once one starts. The court can order disclosure, take a failure into account and order costs, and can set aside orders where a failure to disclose has caused a miscarriage of justice.
  Proposed: The court can order disclosure, take the failure into account and order costs, and it can set aside orders where a failure to disclose has caused a miscarriage of justice. Each of you owes the other full and frank disclosure while preparing for a property case, and owes it to the court as well once a case starts.
- [low] clarity: 'Or go to court' hangs from 'help', so it reads as though our lawyers help clients go to court rather than run the case for them, and the list switches from 'and' to 'or' halfway. The lead is then 70 words, the limit.
  Current: From our Box Hill office, our lawyers help separated spouses and de facto partners divide property, superannuation and debts, and make the settlement binding by consent orders or a binding financial agreement, or go to court when agreement is not possible.
  Proposed: From our Box Hill office, our lawyers help separated spouses and de facto partners divide property, superannuation and debts, make the settlement binding by consent orders or a binding financial agreement, and run the case in court when agreement is not possible.
- [low] fees: 'Per party' can read as a charge to each of you, while the FAQ below says each person needs independent advice and we act for one of you alone; a worried reader may think the agreement costs $6,050 with us. The figure is unchanged, as the gate requires.
  Current: A binding financial agreement drafted from terms you have agreed, with the advice the law requires, is from $3,025 per party including GST.
  Proposed: For a binding financial agreement drafted from terms you have agreed, with the advice the law requires, our fee is from $3,025 including GST for the person we act for, and the other party needs a lawyer of their own.
- [low] consistency: The eyebrow above it says 'Your first call', the free first contact is a call, and the hub, the rail card and the divorce and parenting pages speak of the first call; 'bring' and 'appointment' tell the reader to expect a meeting.
  Current: What to bring to a first appointment.
  Proposed: What to have with you when you call.
- [low] links: The page never links the divorce service page, although a reader asking this question usually needs both. Words unchanged, link added.
  Current: The divorce still matters, because a married couple's 12 month limit runs from the date the divorce order takes effect.
  Proposed: The [divorce](/service-divorce) still matters, because a married couple's 12 month limit runs from the date the divorce order takes effect.

Then: The court can order a child's return to a parent and make orders to stop a child being taken out of Australia, and it can excuse family dispute resolution where an application is urgent. If you are in immediate danger, call 000.
- [medium] voice: 'Your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer' closes the lead, sits in the hero line directly beneath it, opens the second paragraph of the what to bring section and heads the rail card: four times on one page, which reads as padding. The lead's last 11 words would do more work stating the step every application needs, taken word for word from the In brief box. Delete the same sentence from the what to bring section.

Where agreement is not possible, we put your case to the court. Your first call is free, and you speak with a lawyer.

Where agreement is not possible, we put your case to the court. An application for parenting orders generally needs a family dispute resolution certificate, unless the court grants an exemption.
- [medium] voice: Under 'What we do in parenting matters', the first block is a noun phrase with no verb, never says what we do, and shifts from 'the parents' to 'each of you' inside one sentence.
  Current: A written agreement between the parents, signed, dated and made free of any threat, duress or coercion, on where the children live, their time with each of you, holidays and decisions. It cannot be enforced as orders can, but a court making orders has regard to the latest plan where that is in the children's best interests.

A plan must be signed and dated, and made free of any threat, duress or coercion. It cannot be enforced as orders can, but a court making orders has regard to the latest plan where that is in the children's best interests.
- [medium] clarity: At 55 words, with 'among others' arriving only at the end, it is the hardest sentence on the page, in an answer a frightened parent may be reading. Every limb is kept.

The court can also grant one where it is satisfied there are reasonable grounds to believe that a party has committed family violence or abused the child, that there is a risk of family violence by a party, or that the child would be at risk of abuse if the application were delayed.

The divorce page's closing line is the better pattern: what to tell us, and what we will explain.
  Current: One call tells you where you stand and what the next step would be.
